### 453. Carry the degraded-retrieval signal into the zero-result case, where the briefing drops it and the agent is left to invent a fallback
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: literature
- **Dependencies**: Task 328

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/literature/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over BimodalLogic:#761 (ladder realization theorem), user-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BIMODALLOGIC: an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store, per the mis-siting correction recorded under this repo's #390 and the authoring hole recorded under #391. All issue-log citations below are entry_ids in the ORIGINATING repository's log, BimodalLogic:specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl -- the identifier #761 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today (#401); where it matters below it is stated in prose.

CITATION: BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1791766528048_mRmvAU -- class 'tooling bug or gap', severity minor, phase research, dispatch_seq 1, resolution 'worked_around'.

PROVENANCE OF THE FIGURES BELOW: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE sections was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-10-18), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narratives and measured costs are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issues.jsonl entries; they are not re-measurable from this repository, since the incidents happened in BimodalLogic.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- `literature-search.sh` returned degraded=true with NO results for a quasimodel query, and the research agent fell back to grepping the corpus source directory directly for the specific theorem and definition numbers it needed. The workaround succeeded, so nothing was lost on this run; what the entry records is that the agent had to invent the fallback, because the tool told it retrieval had degraded and then told it nothing about what to do next.

ROOT CAUSE -- the degraded signal is produced honestly and then dropped in exactly the case where it is most actionable. Verified in the source store at creation time:
- scripts/literature-search.sh computes `degraded = fallback_tier != 'bm25'` and emits it in the result envelope alongside fallback_tier and query_error. Its own comment states the intent precisely: degraded is "the honest, never-silent signal that distinguishes 'retrieval degraded' from 'genuine zero-result', which literature-briefing.sh consumes downstream". The producer is correct and this record does not fault it.
- When the merged result set is EMPTY, that same code sets `fallback_tier = 'none'`, which makes `degraded` TRUE. So the zero-result case is reported as degraded by construction -- which is the honest reading (no tier answered), but it also means degraded=true with zero results is the single most common degraded shape, not an edge case.
- scripts/literature-briefing.sh is the consumer, and its degraded banner is emitted under the guard `if [ "$degraded" = "true" ] && [ "${#briefing_lines[@]}" -gt 0 ]`. The second conjunct is the defect: the banner requires that there BE results. It carries three well-written variants -- a trigram-fallback warning, a phrase-retry warning after an FTS5 syntax error, and a generic fallback_tier warning -- and each ends by telling the agent to verify relevance itself, which is advice that only makes sense when rows exist.
- Net: in the cited case (degraded=true, fallback_tier='none', zero rows) the briefing emits NO banner and NO directive at all. The signal the producer went to trouble to make never-silent is silent precisely when the agent has nothing else to go on.

SCOPE -- give the zero-result degraded case its own banner and a usable directive, in literature-briefing.sh. The directive should say what the agent in the cited incident worked out by itself, so the next agent does not have to:
- distinguish 'the corpus has nothing on this' from 'the index did not answer this query', and say which one the tool can and cannot tell apart;
- name the sanctioned next steps rather than leaving them to invention -- at minimum re-querying with narrower or alternative terms (the --multi mode exists for per-term recall in one process), consulting --toc / --doc to navigate by document structure instead of ranking, and, as the explicit last resort, reading the corpus source directly;
- state whether an index-health problem is indicated and where that is checked (`/literature --validate`, and the index-health mode of `/literature`).
Also rule on whether `fallback_tier = 'none'` on an empty result set should remain folded into `degraded`, or be reported as its own distinguishable state. Do not change the producer's semantics without recording the reasoning: downstream consumers read the field, and the current definition is deliberate.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Preserve the three existing banner variants verbatim in their current cases (trigram_fallback, phrase_retry, generic). This record ADDS a fourth case; it does not rewrite the working ones.
- Preserve backward compatibility of the envelope reader. literature-briefing.sh already accepts a legacy bare-array shape and two envelope shapes; whatever lands must keep all three readable and must not fail when degraded/fallback_tier/query_error are absent.
- `query_error` is a DIFFERENT condition from `degraded` -- the producer documents it as non-null only when every sub-query failed against every database that exists. A genuine query error must not be collapsed into the new zero-result banner; if anything it deserves a louder one.
- The banner is guidance, not a gate. Nothing here may make a research dispatch fail or block on degraded retrieval.
- No task-number references in the banner text or the context file -- it lands under agent-system/**.

VERIFY:
- Add a literature/scripts/tests/ case covering the cited shape: degraded=true, fallback_tier='none', zero rows -> a banner and a directive ARE emitted. Pin the negative too: a healthy bm25 answer with rows emits NO banner.
- Pin that each of the three existing variants still fires in its own case with its existing wording.
- Pin that all three accepted envelope shapes still parse, including the legacy bare array with no degraded field.
- Reproduce the cited case end-to-end if the corpus allows: a query that no tier answers should now produce actionable text rather than silence.

TERRITORY EDGES:
- 328 (SERIALIZATION, NOT liftable without a read): its file_scope names literature/scripts/literature-search.sh EXPLICITLY (not merely by directory) and also covers literature/scripts/tests/ at directory level, as part of a net-deletion corpus consolidation. This is a direct, named collision on one of this record's own files, so the edge stands until an operator reads 328's plan and confirms otherwise.
- 437 (NO EDGE, checked explicitly): also a literature-extension record, but its file_scope is the Zotero export/search path (zotero-generate-export.sh, zotero-search.sh, zotero-export-status.sh, zotero-export-freshness.sh, tools/zotero-scripts.md). Disjoint files and a disjoint mechanism -- a false-negative acquisition answer from a lossy sqlite reconstruction, not a dropped retrieval-health banner. Both concern honest reporting of a degraded literature answer, so keep the wording consistent, but neither blocks the other.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. No open task names literature-briefing.sh. 404 (completed, literature) was checked. 437 is the only adjacent open literature record and is explicitly ruled disjoint above. No absorption candidate found.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- a plan demanded an absolute gate result the tree cannot produce, so a correctly-passing phase had to report a non-conforming verdict:
(a) "repo-wide check-task-references.sh --quiet exits 0". MEASURED: it exits 1 on 198 pre-existing occurrences in Boneyard/*/README.md (archived code), NONE in any file the task touched; every touched file passes individually and the per-file checks pass. The criterion is unattainable without an out-of-scope archive cleanup.
(b) "vacuous-definition grep count 0", asserted repeatedly across phases. MEASURED: the tree carries one PRE-EXISTING hit at HEAD -- FormalSystem/Examples/TemporalStructures.lean:495, int_domain_universal -- whose `:= trivial` closes a REAL goal (membership in the universal domain is True by definition). So the hit is not even a finding; it is a correct declaration that matches the pattern.
Both had to be reported as baseline-unchanged rather than as the stated criterion. The cost is not a wrong result -- it is that a conforming phase produces a verdict that does not match its own plan, which is indistinguishable on first read from a phase that failed its gate, and so costs a triage pass and erodes the verdict's meaning.

ROOT CAUSE -- the authoring standard has every adjacent rule except this one. Verified in the source store at creation time (around these lines; re-derive before editing):
- context/formats/plan-format.md's `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria` (around line 498) is the closest existing rule and is strikingly near-miss. It MANDATES naming the repository's sanctioned entry point rather than a bare binary, and it MANDATES checking the invoked command's exit status rather than relying on a stdout diff against a baseline -- because "a tool broken identically on both sides of that diff makes 'no new findings' vacuously true". So the file already reasons about baselines, and already warns about one specific way a baseline-relative criterion goes wrong, while never stating when a criterion MUST be baseline-relative in the first place. Its declared enforcement level is advisory prose only, with no corresponding validate-artifact.sh check.
- `### Counts-are-hypotheses obligation` (around line 422) makes any count, file list, or scope estimate a hypothesis carrying a `**Scope Hypothesis**:` line stating how to confirm it at implementation time. That is the right shape for a FORWARD-LOOKING estimate; a standing pre-existing finding count is a different object -- a measurable property of HEAD that should be measured ONCE at plan time and then held fixed as the comparison point.
- `## Verification Tiers` (around line 260) defines how BROADLY verification must run (prose < local < interface < full) and requires the final phase to take the `full` tier, whose in-phase verification is "the complete gate set for the repository". So the format compels a repo-wide run at the final phase while saying nothing about how to phrase the criterion for a repo-wide gate that is not green at HEAD. The two halves do not meet.
- A grep of plan-format.md for baseline / pre-existing / unchanged finds only incidental uses: the stdout-diff warning above, and tier prose using 'unchanged' in an unrelated sense. There is no rule to amend here -- there is a rule to ADD, sited next to the entry-point rule it completes.

SCOPE -- add the baseline-relative criterion rule to plan-format.md, sited as a sibling of `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria`, and propagate it to the planner's own contract. The rule must state at least:
- WHEN it binds: whenever a phase's verification criterion invokes a gate whose scope exceeds the phase's edit scope (a repo-wide lint, a tree-wide grep, an aggregate sweep) AND that gate is not green at the task's starting commit.
- WHAT the criterion must then say: a baseline-relative verdict -- 'no NEW findings relative to the recorded baseline', or 'baseline-unchanged at N' -- never an absolute 'exits 0' or 'count is 0'.
- WHERE the baseline comes from: measured at PLAN time against the task's starting commit and recorded IN the plan, so the implementer compares against a number the plan committed to rather than one it discovers mid-phase and could rationalize. Pair this with the existing counts-are-hypotheses and carried-figure obligations rather than restating them.
- The ESCAPE HATCH, stated explicitly: an absolute criterion remains correct and preferred when the gate IS green at the starting commit, and a plan may legitimately demand an absolute result as a deliberate in-scope cleanup -- in which case the cleanup is part of the task's scope and must appear in a phase's Files to modify.
Then rule on enforcement: whether validate-artifact.sh can mechanically flag an absolute criterion naming a known repo-wide gate, or whether this stays advisory prose like its sibling rule. Record the ruling either way; a mechanical check here is only worth having if it can avoid false-flagging the legitimate absolute case above.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT make this a license to lower a gate. 'No new findings' must mean the gate still RUNS at full repo scope and its exit status is still checked -- the sibling entry-point rule is explicit that a stdout-diff-only comparison makes 'no new findings' vacuously true when the tool is broken identically on both sides. Whatever wording lands must preserve that warning and must not be satisfiable by a diff alone.
- Do NOT restate the carried-figure or counts-are-hypotheses conventions. report-format.md's `## Carried-Figure Discipline` is the authoritative home for carried figures for BOTH reports and plans, and plan-format.md already POINTS there rather than restating, deliberately so the two cannot drift. A recorded baseline figure is a measurement this plan makes, so say how it interacts with those conventions and point; do not fork them.
- Keep every existing heading string in plan-format.md unchanged; several have consumers (validate-artifact.sh, plan-file-scope-harvest.sh reads `Files to modify`, and the lean-gated `## Lean Challenge Statements` section).
- If a mechanical check is added, it MUST fail open and advisory-first. A false flag on a legitimate absolute criterion would push planners toward vaguer criteria, which is worse than the defect being fixed.

VERIFY:
- If a validate-artifact.sh check lands: extend scripts/tests/test-validate-artifact.sh with both polarities -- an absolute criterion over a repo-wide gate is flagged; an absolute criterion over a gate that is green at HEAD, and one paired with an in-scope cleanup phase, are NOT flagged.
- Pin that the existing `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria` and `### Counts-are-hypotheses obligation` sections are intact and that plan-format.md still points to report-format.md for carried figures rather than restating it.
- Use the two cited cases as the acceptance fixtures: a criterion phrased the new way must be satisfiable by the measured states described above (198 archived occurrences untouched; one pre-existing correct declaration matching the vacuous-definition pattern).
- Do NOT verify by running the full verify-deploy.sh aggregator while other sessions are active -- use the scoped narrowing.

FURTHER CASE (c) -- iss_1793830921380_pXdFr7, class documentation, severity COSTLY, phase research,
dispatch_seq 1. A dispatch's verification gate required that check-module-invariants.sh "must be
green". Re-measured at baseline: it exits 1 for a PRE-EXISTING C16 unusedArguments finding on
RaySummary.mem_cellAt_of_shift_of_hasForwardSlack in LadderChain.lean, unrelated to the dispatched
work, while INV, both C15 assertions and C2 all PASS. The criterion was unsatisfiable as written and
the gate had to be read PER-CHECK rather than by script exit code.
WHY THIS IS NOT JUST A THIRD INSTANCE: cases (a) and (b) above are both gates with a standing
FINDING COUNT, where "no new findings relative to the recorded baseline" is the natural wording.
This gate is a multi-check AGGREGATOR whose single exit code conflates independent checks, so a
count-based baseline does not express it -- the operable form is per-check assertion ("INV, C15 and
C2 PASS; C16 baseline-unchanged"). The wording this record adds must therefore cover BOTH shapes, or
it will leave the aggregator case without a sanctioned phrasing. Nothing above currently does.

FURTHER CASE (d) -- iss_1793830921344_wcU6Ej, class documentation, severity minor, phase research,
dispatch_seq 1. The INVERSE failure, from the same baseline measurement. A widely-repeated dispatch
note stated that check-metalogic-cycles.sh currently exits 1 for a pre-existing eighth-upward-edge
reason. Re-measured at baseline: it exits 0, printing "PASS upward import set is exactly the
recorded 8 line(s)". The note was stale, and the staleness inverted the correct reading -- under the
true baseline a nonzero exit is a NEW failure, which the stale note would have had an implementer
dismiss as pre-existing.
This is the direct argument for this record's "WHERE the baseline comes from" clause, and it
sharpens it: a baseline inherited as DISPATCH PROSE decays silently and in the dangerous direction
(toward dismissing real failures), whereas a baseline measured at plan time against the task's
starting commit cannot. State that asymmetry in the rule rather than only requiring plan-time
measurement, so the reason the prose route is forbidden survives in the text.

- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: lean-extension
- **Dependencies**: Task 385, Task 420, Task 435

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/lean/** (primarily) and agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today (#401); where it matters below it is stated in prose.

CITATIONS -- two entries, one cause, seven days apart:
  BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1792362555851_OvmVEP  (class tooling-refusal, severity minor, implement, dispatch_seq 6) -- the refusal itself.
  BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1791770495965_TM78BN  (class gate-baseline, severity minor, implement, dispatch_seq 3) -- the downstream consequence, recorded FIRST and independently.

PROVENANCE OF THE FIGURES BELOW: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE sections was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-10-18), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narratives and measured costs are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issues.jsonl entries; they are not re-measurable from this repository, since the incidents happened in BimodalLogic.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- the snapshot is unobtainable by the time anything asks for it, so a per-phase drift check degrades to hand-diffing. Per the cited entries:
- `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh 761 .` refused with exit 73 because the task's status was already 'implementing'. The plan's Phase 1 had asked for the snapshot precisely so that `--check` would be available to the later phases.
- THE REFUSAL IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE WEAKENED. The implementer declined to pass --force, on the stated ground that the tool's own banner says a post-implementation Challenge certifies nothing, so a forced manifest would be MISLEADING PROVENANCE in git history. That judgment is right and this record endorses it.
- Consequence, from the earlier entry: `--check` exited 71 at a later phase (no manifest at the task directory's challenge/manifest.json, because the snapshot was never taken at plan time), so the advisory drift check could not run for ANY phase. Three Goal signatures were instead diffed byte-for-byte against the plan's Lean Challenge Statements block by hand. They matched -- so nothing was certified wrongly; the loss is that the mechanical check was replaced by a manual one that does not scale and leaves no artifact.

ROOT CAUSE -- the snapshot has no owner in the lifecycle. Verified in the source store at creation time:
- scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh hard-codes PRE_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUSES="not_started researching researched planning planned" and refuses every later status (implementing, pr_ready, completed, blocked, partial, abandoned, expanded) with exit 73, on the documented ground that "a Challenge must predate implementation to certify anything". --force exists only as a loud, incident-shaped bypass, never a silent one. All of this is correct.
- The producing side exists too: context/formats/plan-format.md carries `## Lean Challenge Statements (format, lean/lean4 plans only)`, whose own text says the section exists "so a downstream tool (lean-challenge-snapshot.sh) can turn a plan into a trusted, immutable Challenge module BEFORE the implementation agent runs".
- NOTHING INVOKES IT. extensions/lean/manifest.json has a top-level `hooks` value of null -- the lean extension registers no lifecycle hooks at any stage. So the only thing that ever asks for a snapshot is a plan that writes "take the snapshot" into a phase body, which by construction executes during IMPLEMENT, which is exactly the status the gate refuses. The design record already identifies the plan as the authoritative Challenge source (its R1-vs-R2 ruling makes plan-declared statements primary and git-baseline extraction a loudly-degraded fallback for legacy plans only), so the data is in hand at plan time; only the invocation is missing.

SCOPE -- make the plan phase's own postflight take the snapshot for lean/lean4 tasks whose plan carries a non-empty `## Lean Challenge Statements` section. Determine the right registration point and record why:
(a) a `postflight` lifecycle hook declared in extensions/lean/manifest.json's top-level `hooks` object (see docs/guides/creating-extensions.md for the hook schema and execution contract) -- extension-local, which is where lean-specific behaviour belongs, but note that the lean extension currently registers no hooks at all, so this establishes the pattern and must be verified to actually fire;
(b) a gated call from core's plan-cycle postflight (scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh) -- guaranteed to run, but puts lean-specific knowledge in core, which is the wrong direction unless (a) is demonstrated unworkable.
Prefer (a); fall back to (b) only with the reason recorded.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- DO NOT weaken the exit-73 status gate, and do not add an automatic --force anywhere. The gate is the reason the refusal was trustworthy; a snapshot taken at the right time makes the gate irrelevant rather than wrong.
- The hook MUST be non-blocking. A plan phase must never fail because a snapshot could not be taken. Absence of a manifest already degrades gracefully -- `--check` exits 71 and the drift check is advisory -- so the correct failure mode is a warning plus no manifest, exactly as today.
- The hook MUST be inert for a task that is not lean/lean4, and inert for a lean4 plan that commits to no theorem. THAT SECOND CASE IS NOT YET DEFINED: #385 exists precisely to define the sanctioned shape for a zero-theorem lean4 plan and to make this same script distinguish it from a malformed one. Until that ruling lands, a hook added here would fire on exactly the undefined case. This is why #385 is a hard dependency, not a courtesy edge.
- Idempotence and immutability: re-running the plan postflight (a revision, a second plan cycle) must not silently overwrite an existing Challenge manifest. The design record's immutability mechanism is load-bearing -- a Challenge the agent can rewrite certifies nothing. Decide and record what a re-plan does: refuse, version, or require an explicit regeneration.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh (and/or add a hook test): the hook fires for a lean4 task with a populated Challenge section and produces a manifest under the task directory's challenge/ subdirectory; a later `--check` from an implement phase then exits 0 rather than 71.
- Pin that the hook is inert for a non-lean task type, and that a plan-phase hook failure does not fail the plan phase.
- Pin that the exit-73 status gate and its PRE_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUSES set are UNCHANGED, and that --force still warns in its incident-shaped form.
- Pin the re-plan behaviour chosen above in both directions.

TERRITORY EDGES:
- 385 (HARD SEMANTIC DEPENDENCY, not merely serialization): same two lean files (lean-challenge-snapshot.sh and its design record challenge-snapshot.md) plus core/context/formats/plan-format.md. It defines the sanctioned shape for a lean4 plan that commits to no theorem and makes the script distinguish it from a malformed one. A hook added before that ruling fires on an undefined case and will mis-handle it. 385 MUST land first.
- No edge on 328: its file_scope covers lean/agents/, lean/commands/, lean/rules/ and lean/skills/ but NOT lean/scripts/ or lean/scripts/tests/, so there is no collision with this record's targets. Checked explicitly rather than assumed.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 385 is the only open task touching these files and is a dependency rather than a duplicate -- it governs WHICH plans the tool must accept; this record governs WHEN the tool is called. 396 and 412 (lean-extension) were checked and do not name these files. No absorption candidate found.

- 435 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/ coarsely, which covers this record's one core target, scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh (relevant only if registration option (b) is chosen; if option (a), the manifest hook, is taken, this edge is moot and should be lifted).

CITATION: BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1792373673886_BRjcBd -- severity BLOCKING, class 'orchestration defect', phase implement, dispatch_seq 11, suggested_channel agent_system. The single highest-severity entry in that log and the highest-value item in the batch this record came from.

PROVENANCE OF THE FIGURES BELOW: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE sections was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-10-18), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narratives and measured costs are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issues.jsonl entries; they are not re-measurable from this repository, since the incidents happened in BimodalLogic.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- a blocking user_decision was raised, answered, recorded, and acted on, and the dispatch that acted on it closed TWO phases [BLOCKED] on a second, independent obstruction the chosen route does not touch. Per the cited entry: the settled decision fixed a phase merge (run a later phase before an earlier one); the dispatch entered that merged phase and hit a circularity the merge does not address -- one goal's obligation needs a constancy property that is itself a third phase's goal, while that third phase derives its own result from the first two, so three phases are mutually circular as decomposed. The plan's own recorded finding states the same dependency in the opposite direction, so the circularity was latent in the artifacts BEFORE the question was asked. Nothing was silently damaged -- no library file changed, no sorry, axiom, or vacuous definition written, and the obstruction was landed as structured BLOCKER records plus two standalone-compiled lemmas quoted in the handoff. The loss is the cycle, not the tree.

WHY THIS IS A MECHANISM DEFECT AND NOT AN AGENT FAULT. The decision pipeline is complete and correct at every step EXCEPT that no step ever checks the answer against the obligation it is supposed to discharge. Verified in the source store at creation time:
- scripts/orchestrate-record-decision.sh is the SINGLE sanctioned writer for a task's specs/{NNN}_{slug}/.decisions.json. Its required arguments are exactly --task, --session, --cycle, --question, --answer. Its own header states the schema it enforces, per docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md's "Decisions File Schema" section: a bare top-level JSON array with FOUR required fields per entry (question, answer, cycle [integer], timestamp [ISO 8601 UTC]). There is NO field for the obligation the answer is meant to discharge, so the claim is not merely unchecked -- it is unrecordable.
- scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh emits the answer to the next dispatch under a `## Prior Decisions` heading whose own instruction is "Treat these as settled -- do not re-ask a question already answered here" (and, when the description has since changed, a POTENTIALLY SUPERSEDED variant deferring to the current `## Description`). Settled-ness is therefore propagated as an instruction not to re-litigate, with nothing propagated that the receiving dispatch could check the route AGAINST.
- context/standards/user-decision-contract.md governs when an agent sets `user_decision` and how postflight relays it. A grep of that file for route / unblock / obligation / discharge / validate returns NOTHING: the contract constrains WHEN to ask and the field's shape, never what a proposed option must claim about what it fixes.
- docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md carries exactly one decision-related section heading, "Prior Decisions vs. Current Description: Precedence" -- a precedence rule for conflict between two records, not a validity check on either.
Net: the route arrived at the implementer carrying the full authority of a user decision and none of the checkability. An unfalsifiable instruction is the thing to fix.

SCOPE -- choose and implement ONE mechanism, recording the reasoning and why the others were rejected. The candidates below are starting points, NOT a prescription, and they are not mutually exclusive:
(1) Make the claim recordable, then require it. Add an obligation field to the decisions schema (handoff-schema.md plus a new orchestrate-record-decision.sh argument and its test), and amend user-decision-contract.md so an option offered as a ROUTE must name the specific obligation it discharges. Cheapest and most durable; its weakness is that a named obligation is still only a claim until something reads it.
(2) Validate between recording and dispatching. Insert a cheap check in skill-orchestrate's own flow -- after the answer is recorded, before the dispatch is composed -- that the named obligation is actually discharged by the chosen route, and refuse or re-ask when it is not. This is the only option that catches the cited failure BEFORE a cycle is spent. Its cost is that 'actually discharged' must be made operable; for the cited case the operable form is cheap and specific, since the plan's own recorded finding already stated the contrary dependency and a read of the plan's findings would have surfaced it.
(3) Propagate the claim to the implementer instead of validating centrally. Emit the named obligation alongside the answer in `## Prior Decisions`, with an explicit instruction to verify the route discharges it before beginning phase work and to return a blocking user_decision immediately if it does not. Weakest guarantee, but it survives a route whose validity is only determinable with the phase's own context in hand.
Prefer the combination (1)+(2) if the validation in (2) can be made cheap; fall back to (1)+(3) if it cannot. Record the ruling either way.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- The schema change MUST be backward compatible. orchestrate-record-decision.sh's header documents the object-wrapped shape ({"decisions": [...]}) as the observed failure the script exists to make unreachable, and documents that a pre-existing object-wrapped file is REFUSED rather than silently coerced. Any new field must be OPTIONAL, must leave existing four-field entries valid, and must not relax the root-level array-type validation performed both before and after merge.
- Preserve the script's write mechanics exactly: flock -x held across the entire read -> merge -> validate -> atomic-mv sequence, additive-only append, and a byte-identical pre-existing file on any failure. Do not introduce a second writer; this script is documented as the single sanctioned one.
- Do NOT weaken the do-not-re-ask instruction into a license to re-litigate a settled question. The defect is that a route is unchecked, not that it is settled. A validation step that fails must surface a NEW, specific blocking question naming the second obstruction -- never re-ask the original.
- Keep the heading string `## Prior Decisions` and the existing POTENTIALLY SUPERSEDED marker semantics unchanged; both already have consumers.
- Fail OPEN. A route whose validity cannot be determined mechanically must dispatch with a warning, never block. The cost of a false refusal here is a stalled orchestration; the cost of a false pass is the already-measured one cycle.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-record-decision.sh: a four-field legacy entry still validates and still appends; an entry carrying the new field round-trips; an object-wrapped file is still refused with the pre-existing file left byte-identical; the flock/atomic-mv sequence is unchanged.
- Extend scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh to pin whatever is newly emitted, and to pin that `## Prior Decisions` and the POTENTIALLY SUPERSEDED variant are both still emitted with their existing heading and wording.
- Construct a regression fixture from the cited shape: a recorded answer whose route does not discharge a stated obligation, where the contrary dependency is already present in the plan's own findings. Whichever mechanism is chosen must surface that case; pin it.
- Do NOT verify by running the full verify-deploy.sh aggregator while other sessions are active -- use the scoped narrowing (--only-gate N, or scripts/tests/run-all.sh --only SUITE).

TERRITORY EDGES (each labelled with its rationale; declared per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md):
- 447 (SERIALIZATION plus a real SEMANTIC edge): also writes scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and its test. Its PART B extends a carried-figure obligation to "a dispatch's acted-on premise" -- the nearest existing work to this record, and plausibly the same mechanism viewed from the artifact-discipline side rather than the decision side. RECONCILE THE TWO DURING RESEARCH and record the ruling: if 447's premise re-validation checklist already covers a settled route as an acted-on premise, this record narrows to the schema and contract half (candidate 1) and says so explicitly.
- 449 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and its test, and is itself deciding the emitted SECTION ORDER of that dispatch file. If this record adds or moves a section there, 449's ordering decision must land first.

Where this record's live symptom is a route that was SETTLED but did not discharge its obligation,

WHY IT BELONGS ON THIS RECORD. The common defect is the one this record already names as the thing
to fix: a route reaches a dispatch carrying authority and no checkability. The two polarities are
an option set asserted to have a valid member that does not, and an option set asserted to have
several members when it has one. A mechanism that makes a route's claim recordable and checkable
(candidate 1) or that validates it before dispatch (candidate 2) should be expressible over both,
and an obligation field that can only describe what a chosen route discharges will not catch the
second. Check that when choosing the mechanism, and if candidate (1) or (2) lands, add this shape as

### 449. Site the Batch Gate Deferral directive where it is read before the phase's verification is launched
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 360, Task 407, Task 414, Task 444, Task 445, Task 446, Task 448

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over tasks 417, 424, 443 and 413, session sess_1792311788_aadaee. User-approved at the lead level.

CITATION: #417:iss_1792315896632_CrEg7F

LIVE SYMPTOM: a phase ran the complete verify-deploy.sh aggregator instead of the scoped narrowing (--only-gate N, or scripts/tests/run-all.sh --only SUITE) that its OWN dispatch file directed for exactly that phase, because the task was part of a multi-task batch. The implementer's own account is that the full run was already in flight before it re-read the directive's section.

MEASURED COST of that one miss: 9 of 38 checks failed as contention timeouts, under a 1-minute load average of 52.70 on a 24-core host with 21 concurrent gate processes. That is precisely the noise the directive exists to prevent -- and it is indistinguishable, on first read, from a real regression, so it also costs a triage pass.

ROOT CAUSE -- the directive is sited where it is read too late to be honoured. Verified in the source store while creating this task (re-derive before editing; line numbers move):
- scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh emits the dispatch file's sections in this fixed order (the `echo "## ..."` call sites, with approximate lines): `# Dispatch Context` header 510, `## Identity` 516, `## Description` 533, `## Artifact Round` 541, `## Research Artifact` 548, `## Prior Plan` 553, `## Plan` 561, `## Continuation` 582, `## Phase Mission` 594, `## Batch Gate Deferral` 605, `## Handoff` 627, `## Issue Log` 639, `## Territory` 672, `## Prior Decisions` 719, `## Wait Discipline` 737, `## User-Decision Contract` 747.
- `## Batch Gate Deferral` is therefore the TENTH of sixteen sections, and it sits immediately after `## Plan` -- the single largest section in a typical dispatch file, since it carries the plan body. A reader working the file top-down reaches the plan, and the phase mission, before reaching the directive that constrains how the phase's verification must be run.
- agents/general-implementation-agent.md around lines 469-470 states the consuming obligation as "**Batch Gate Deferral (final phase only)**: before running this phase's verification, check whether the dispatch file (`.dispatch/{seq}.md`) carries a `## Batch Gate Deferral` section." So the obligation is correctly written and correctly placed in the agent's Stage 4 -- the agent file is not the defect. Line 1023 of the same file carries the paired anti-pattern ("Treat a `## Batch Gate Deferral` section as license to skip verification entirely"), pointing back at the Stage 4 carve-out.
- context/contracts/phase-closure.md around line 186 and context/formats/plan-format.md also reference the section by name; scripts/orchestrate-batch-gate.sh is the batch-gate stage itself, and context/contracts/batch-gate-stage.md is its contract.

SCOPE -- choose and implement ONE mechanism, with the reasoning recorded. Candidate directions, offered as starting points and NOT as a prescription; they are not mutually exclusive:
(1) Hoist the section. Emit `## Batch Gate Deferral` ahead of `## Plan` -- near `## Identity` or immediately after `## Description` -- so it cannot be passed over. Cheapest change; weigh it against the dispatch file's existing narrative order, in which identity and description precede the work and constraints follow it.
(2) Restate it at the point of use. Repeat the constraint inside `## Phase Mission` (or in the final phase's mission specifically), so the directive appears where the phase's verification is actually being planned. Weigh the drift risk of a second copy; if chosen, one site must be documented as authoritative and the other as derived.
(3) Make the aggregator itself batch-aware. Have verify-deploy.sh (or the dispatch-side wrapper) detect an active multi-task batch and refuse, warn, or auto-narrow to the scoped form. This is the only option that survives a reader who never reads the section at all, and so is the most robust -- but it is also the largest change and must not break a legitimate standalone full run.
Record which option was chosen and why the others were not.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT turn the deferral into a license to skip verification. agents/general-implementation-agent.md's line-1023 anti-pattern is explicit on this point and must remain true after the change. The deferral narrows the run; it never removes it.
- Keep the section's NAME exactly `## Batch Gate Deferral`. At least four consumers match on that heading string (the agent file's Stage 4 check and its anti-pattern list, context/contracts/phase-closure.md, context/formats/plan-format.md). A rename breaks all of them silently.
- The final-phase-only scoping of the obligation is deliberate. Do not widen it to every phase without stating why; the cited miss was a Phase 4 of a longer plan, so determine first whether the directive even applied there, and record that finding. If it did not, the defect is the scoping, not the siting, and the fix changes accordingly.
- If option (3) is chosen, a standalone (non-batch) full aggregator run must remain possible and unchanged, and the detection must fail OPEN -- an undetectable batch state must never block a verification run.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh with a fixture pinning the section's emitted POSITION (not merely its presence) relative to `## Plan` and `## Phase Mission`, so a later reordering cannot silently undo this.
- Pin that the heading string is unchanged, and that every consumer named above still matches it.
- If option (3) is chosen, pin both directions: a batch context narrows or refuses, and a standalone context runs the full aggregator unchanged.
- Do NOT verify this record by running the full verify-deploy.sh aggregator while other sessions are active -- that is the exact contention this record exists to prevent. Use the scoped narrowing.

TERRITORY EDGES (declared per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md, each labelled with its rationale):
- 446 (SERIALIZATION, with a light semantic edge): also writes agents/general-implementation-agent.md and context/contracts/phase-closure.md. Its PART B rewrites the phase-closing verification obligation in exactly those two files; this record changes how a verification-scoping directive reaches the implementer for a phase close. Both land in the same prose; sequencing them avoids one rewriting the other's text.
- 447 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh, because its PART B may put a premise re-validation checklist into the dispatch file. If it adds a section there, this record's section-ordering decision must account for it, so it lands first.
- 445 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/verify-deploy.sh, which this record's option (3) would make batch-aware. That record gives Gate 8 an expected-versus-new failure distinction instead of collapsing any non-zero run-all.sh exit to FAIL -- closely related in subject (both concern how a full aggregator run reports under adverse conditions) but disjoint in mechanism, and it was created concurrently with this one, so the two were never reconciled at creation time. Reconcile them during research.
- 407 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh. It derives a task's next artifact round from existing artifacts -- no semantic interaction with section siting, but a direct file collision in the composer and its suite.
- 328 (SERIALIZATION only): its file_scope covers agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/, agents/ and context/ at DIRECTORY level and is a net-deletion corpus consolidation that would churn these same files. An operator who confirms it does not touch these specific files may lift this edge with update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 413 (completed) coordinated full gate-set runs across concurrent sessions in one repository -- the nearest neighbour in subject, and the reason the contention cost above is measurable at all. It addressed cross-session coordination of gate runs; this record addresses whether a single implementer READS the directive in time to honour it, which that coordination work does not cover. 427 (completed) fixed a different dispatch-file content defect (a superseded Prior Decision emitted as settled) in the same composer -- same file, disjoint section and cause. No absorption candidate found; edges were declared instead.

### 448. Contain the regression-anchor source-store mutation so a concurrent session cannot observe or inherit it
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 418

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over tasks 417, 424, 443 and 413, session sess_1792311788_aadaee. User-approved at the lead level.

CITATION: #413:iss_1792326370408_zQ17Qn

THIS RECORD IS PREVENTION ONLY. The cited instance was already reverted and redeployed during that invocation with the user's approval, and the mutation is confirmed ABSENT from the source store as of this record's creation (verified: `grep -n "while false" agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` returns nothing, and `find agent-system/extensions/core -name "*.anchor-bak"` returns nothing). Do NOT create or re-do a fix for the already-fixed instance.

LIVE SYMPTOM: an uncommitted working-tree mutation in agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh (around lines 2170 and 2183) had changed `while IFS= read -r f; do` to `while false && IFS= read -r f; do`, neutering the cycle_modified_files and invocation_modified_files accumulators. It was left behind by whichever session had exercised the suite's own deliberate-break technique.
Two distinct consequences observed in a LATER, unrelated invocation:
- It failed scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh inside another task's verification run, where it was initially indistinguishable from a genuine regression -- so it cost a triage pass before it was recognised as foreign.
- It silently degraded that batch's own per-task finding attribution: cycle_modified_files stayed at 0, with no error anywhere.
The second is the worse one: a loud failure gets investigated, a silent zero gets believed.

MECHANICS VERIFIED IN THE SOURCE STORE (re-derive before editing; line numbers move). The technique is legitimate, documented, and deliberately designed -- the defect is its blast radius, not its existence:
- scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh around lines 2568-2612 is the "Regression-anchor check". Its own header comment states the purpose: "temporarily re-gate the WORK (j) accumulation block on a deliberately-false condition, confirm the same fixture goes RED, then revert -- proves this case actually exercises the block rather than passing vacuously."
- It resolves `ANCHOR_SRC="$CORE_DIR/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh"` -- the REAL source-store file in the live working tree -- guards on an anchor comment being present, copies it to `${ANCHOR_SRC}.anchor-bak`, installs `trap 'anchor_restore; cleanup' EXIT`, then runs `sed -i "s/^    while IFS= read -r f; do\$/    while false \&\& IFS= read -r f; do/" "$ANCHOR_SRC"`, runs the fixture, and calls `anchor_restore` followed by `trap cleanup EXIT`.
- The comment explains WHY it must mutate $CORE_DIR rather than the already-copied $SUT: "setup_sandbox always re-copies FROM $CORE_DIR, so the mutation must live there, not on the already-copied $SUT, to survive the next setup_sandbox call". This constraint is real and any fix must address it rather than ignore it.

ROOT CAUSE -- two independent holes, both of which this record must close or explicitly rule out:
(1) An EXIT trap is not a guarantee. It does not fire on SIGKILL, on a host crash, or on an OOM kill; and an operator who interrupts a long suite can land outside it. The backup/restore pair is best-effort recovery, not containment.
(2) Far more important, and the hole the cited evidence actually exercised: even when the trap works perfectly, the mutation is LIVE IN THE SHARED WORKING TREE for the whole duration of that fixture. Any concurrently running session -- another task's verification run, a gate, an unrelated /orchestrate cycle -- reads the broken file during that window and has no way to know why. The observed failure was not a missed revert; it was a concurrency window being observed from outside. A fix that only hardens the revert path leaves the real hazard intact.

SCOPE -- decide and implement a coherent containment rule, with the reasoning recorded. Candidate directions, offered as starting points and NOT as a prescription; they are not mutually exclusive, and the strongest answer likely pairs one containment option with the detector:
(1) Eliminate the shared-tree mutation. Give the harness a way to point setup_sandbox's copy source at a private mutated copy -- an override source directory -- so the deliberate break lives in a throwaway tree and $CORE_DIR is never written. This is the only option that closes hole (2) outright, and it directly answers the constraint the existing comment names. Check scripts/lib/test-harness-lib.sh for where setup_sandbox's source is resolved.
(2) Serialize the window. Hold the repository-level lock or mutex the gate/test infrastructure already uses for the duration of the mutation, so no concurrent reader can observe it. Cheaper, but it only shrinks the window rather than removing it, and it couples suite runtime to that lock.
(3) Add a mechanical detector, independent of whichever containment option is chosen. A `while false &&` guard is never legitimate production code, which is exactly what made this instance recognisable after the fact -- that recognisability should be mechanical rather than retrospective. A leftover `*.anchor-bak` file in the source store is a second, equally unambiguous signal, and a cheaper one. Candidate home: a new check under scripts/lint/ alongside the existing lint-* family, wired into the gate registry.
Record which options were chosen and why the others were not. If option (1) is rejected, the rejection must say how hole (2) is otherwise closed.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT delete or weaken the regression-anchor check. It is the thing that proves the fixture is not passing vacuously, and a vacuously-passing characterization test is worse than no test. Whatever is built must keep the anchor's guarantee intact: breaking the accumulation block must still flip that case red.
- Respect the constraint the existing comment names: setup_sandbox re-copies FROM $CORE_DIR, so a mutation applied to $SUT alone is erased by the next setup_sandbox call. Any option (1) implementation must handle this, not work around it by skipping setup_sandbox.
- A detector must be scoped to the SOURCE STORE and must not fire on the test file's own `sed` pattern string, which legitimately contains the literal `while false &&` text as a substitution replacement. That self-exclusion is the detector's main design problem -- solve it explicitly rather than by a blanket path exclusion that would also blind the detector to a real mutation in a test file.
- The detector must be advisory or gate-level, not a write-time block: a suite legitimately holds the mutation mid-run, so a hard block would make the technique unusable.
- Any new gate or lint must follow the existing scripts/lint/ family's conventions and be registered wherever the existing lint-* checks are registered, rather than added as a free-floating script.

VERIFY:
- Pin the anchor's own guarantee after the change: with the containment in place, artificially re-gating the accumulation block must STILL flip the cited case red. If it no longer does, the containment has broken the anchor and the change is wrong.
- Pin that $CORE_DIR/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh is byte-identical before and after a full run of scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh -- the invariant that the cited instance violated. Also pin that no `*.anchor-bak` survives the run.
- If a detector is added, give it its own suite under scripts/tests/ pinning both directions: it fires on a planted `while false &&` guard and on a planted leftover `*.anchor-bak`, and it does NOT fire on the test file's own legitimate `sed` replacement string.
- Audit scripts/tests/ for any OTHER suite that mutates a source-store file in the shared working tree, and bring each under the same rule or record why it is exempt. Verified while creating this record: scripts/tests/test-validate-no-task-references.sh also uses the phrase "regression anchor" (around lines 193-203) but by a SAFE fixture-based mechanism with no source mutation, so it is not in scope -- confirm no third site exists.

TERRITORY EDGES (declared per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md, each labelled with its rationale):
- 328 (SEMANTIC + file overlap): its file_scope explicitly names agent-system/extensions/core/context/standards/shell-script-testing.md and agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lib/test-harness-lib.sh, and covers scripts/tests/ and scripts/lint/ at directory level. It is a net-deletion corpus consolidation over exactly the suite-authoring surface a containment rule would be written into, and it also lists scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh by name. Landing this record first would mean writing a standard into files that consolidation then churns. Must land first.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 415 was read and rejected as an absorption target: it records exact-pid process checking as a parallelism-safety suite-authoring standard -- the same FAMILY (a suite-authoring standard about parallel safety) but a disjoint mechanism (how a suite identifies a process, not whether a suite mutates shared source). If both land, the two standards should sit together in whichever file 415 chose; note that as a placement preference, not a dependency. 413 (completed) coordinated full gate-set runs across concurrent sessions, which reduces how often two suites overlap but does nothing about a mutation observed during an overlap that does occur.

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over tasks 417, 424, 443 and 413, session sess_1792311788_aadaee. User-approved at the lead level.

CITATIONS -- six entries across three independent tasks in ONE invocation:
  #417:iss_1792313133347_sYEA9t
  #417:iss_1792313199291_fFKK9m
  #424:iss_1792337289912_zl0Jq5
  #413:iss_1792323411434_KyvPGL
  #413:iss_1792326472706_NNRVgW
  #413:iss_1792323496988_DwFozE
The first five record the divergence itself; the sixth records the unread-warning half (see PART B).

LIVE SYMPTOM, PART A -- declared file_scope is narrower than the plan's own "Files to modify" list, so git-commit-phase.sh silently drops real work from phase commits:
- #417's entry records the shape most precisely: file_scope at task-creation time held 5 entries while the plan's Phase 2 "Files to modify" named 8 rule files plus their non-eager destinations. The Containment Self-Check correctly dropped rules/artifact-formats.md; the recovery was to amend state.json's file_scope to the plan's authoritative list and re-run the commit.
- Other dropped paths: .claude/scripts/test-four-tier-conflict.sh (#424), agent-system/extensions/core/index-entries.json (#413), and #413's own summaries/*.md.

LIVE SYMPTOM, PART B -- a drop-and-warn WARNING can go unread for multiple phases, leaving a real deviation fix uncommitted:
- scripts/tests/test-run-all-parallel.sh was dropped from a Phase 2 commit and sat as an uncommitted deviation fix across Phases 2 through 6, undetected until a routine pre-Phase-7 git-status re-check caught it. The same drop happened independently for index-entries.json in Phase 6 but was caught immediately that time, because that phase's commit stdout was actually read.
- The containment check was working exactly as designed in both halves. The failure is that its warning is advisory text nobody is required to consume, and the commit's summary line alone does not confirm containment.
- The recorded lesson: either add the path via the sanctioned research/plan-stage --file-scope-add mechanism before implementing, or explicitly verify the phase-closing commit's WARNING output names every expected file.

THE RECONCILIATION MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTS -- this is the single most important starting fact, and it inverts the obvious framing of PART A. Do NOT build a new reconciler. All of the following was verified in the source store while creating this task (re-derive before editing; line numbers move):

- scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh (362 lines) harvests the deduplicated union of every phase's "Files to modify" path list from a plan file and emits a sorted JSON array. Its header documents the accepted grammar verbatim against context/formats/plan-format.md's field definition: a `**Files to modify**:` / `**Files to modify:**` block header optionally prefixed by a single list marker; a NEW path entry only on a line matching `^[ \t]*-[ \t]*\`` with the path as the first backtick-delimited token; the block ending at the first `###` heading, new bolded field label, or a blank line not immediately followed by another entry. Wrapped continuation lines, prose sentinels, and table cells contribute NOTHING -- by design, not as a parse error. It never filesystem-validates a harvested path (a path for a file the plan will CREATE must be harvested, not filtered).
- scripts/skill-base.sh around lines 1012-1035 is the LIVE plan-postflight harvest call site. Its own comment states this explicitly: it resolves the version-ordered latest plans/*.md, runs plan-file-scope-harvest.sh, and forwards a non-empty result as `--file-scope-add=<json>` into the same update-task-status.sh postflight write, whose union-merge is additive and never removes an entry. scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh sources skill-base.sh (around line 2379), so an /orchestrate plan cycle reaches this path.
- scripts/orchestrator-postflight.sh around lines 330-350 carries a SECOND copy of the same branch, and its own header states that script has no live caller. Its comment at the harvest site says both copies must carry the harvest "not only the orphaned one" -- so the duplication is deliberate, and any change here must touch both or record why not.
- Three other consumers already depend on the same field: scripts/validate-artifact.sh (around lines 330-365) fails a plan in which EVERY phase omits the field, with the explicit message that the harvest would otherwise yield nothing and leave file_scope null; scripts/reconcile-task-status.sh (around lines 216-230) re-runs the harvester as a subprocess; scripts/backfill-file-scope.sh reuses it across a whole state.json. scripts/orchestrate-batch-admit.sh (around lines 218 and 787) points an operator at both scripts when a task has no file_scope.
- scripts/git-commit-phase.sh is the drop site: the warn helper is around line 189, and the `issue-record.sh --class "scope excursion" --severity minor` call with the exact text `git-commit-phase.sh dropped '<path>' from a phase commit pathspec: not contained in this task's declared file_scope.` is around lines 270-280. Its header (around lines 76-90) documents fail-OPEN on a missing/empty/malformed file_scope, and that the post-commit completeness check runs only over SURVIVING paths -- so a containment-dropped path is excluded from the very check that would otherwise catch it as un-landed.

THE REAL QUESTION, therefore: the plan-stage reconciliation is live, additive, and wired into the /orchestrate plan cycle, yet five divergences were still observed in a single invocation. Find out WHY, then close that gap. Candidate causes, every one an UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS to be confirmed or refuted against the four cited tasks' own plan artifacts and state.json history before any edit:
(a) Grammar miss -- the plan rendered those paths in a form the harvester contributes nothing for (a wrapped continuation line, a table, inline prose, or a bare path with no backticks). #417's "8 rule files plus their non-eager destinations" is the sharpest test case: read that plan's Phase 2 block and run the harvester against it.
(b) Path-form mismatch -- the harvested string and the containment predicate's input are in different forms (deploy-relative `rules/artifact-formats.md` versus repo-relative `agent-system/extensions/core/rules/artifact-formats.md`). #417's dropped path is recorded in the SHORT form and #413's in the LONG form, which is direct circumstantial evidence for this hypothesis.
(c) Ordering -- the plan was authored, revised, or extended AFTER the plan postflight that harvested it, so the harvest read an earlier plan body. Note the harvester reads only the version-ordered LATEST plans/*.md.
(d) The plan's own list was genuinely incomplete, and the implementer discovered the path mid-phase. This is the case 430 owns; if the evidence lands here, say so and defer to that record rather than duplicating it.
(e) The harvest ran and merged correctly, but something later narrowed or overwrote file_scope.
Record the verdict per cited entry. A fix aimed at the wrong cause is worse than none here, because the mechanism it would duplicate already exists.

SCOPE -- decide and implement ONE coherent rule for each part, with the reasoning recorded:
PART A: close the specific gap the evidence names. If the cause is (a) or (b), the fix is in the harvester's grammar or in a shared path-normalization applied to BOTH the harvest output and the containment predicate's input -- not in widening file_scope by hand. If it is (c), the fix is in when the harvest runs or in re-running it. State which cause each cited entry is attributed to.
PART B: make the drop non-advisory. Options to weigh, not a prescription: surface the dropped set as a structured field a phase-closing check must consume (rather than stdout text a reader may miss); make the phase-closure contract require naming every expected file against the commit's actual WARNING output; or carry the dropped set into the progress/handoff record so the NEXT phase sees an outstanding drop rather than relying on the current phase's reader. Whatever is chosen must still work when the drop is correct (see HARD CONSTRAINTS).

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- #424's dropped path was .claude/scripts/test-four-tier-conflict.sh -- under the DEPLOYED tree, where dropping is CORRECT behaviour per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md. No reconciliation may "fix" that case by widening file_scope into .claude/**. A plan that names a .claude/** path in its "Files to modify" list is itself the defect in that instance; decide explicitly whether the harvester, validate-artifact.sh, or neither should refuse such a path, and record the ruling.
- Do NOT weaken the containment predicate for a genuine excursion. Drop-and-warn for an out-of-territory source edit is correct and must survive intact, pinned by a test.
- Do NOT make the check fatal, and do NOT remove fail-open on a missing/empty/unreadable/malformed file_scope. Both are deliberate.
- Preserve the additive, never-remove semantics of update-task-status.sh's --file-scope-add union-merge, and route any state.json write through the existing mutex-guarded state-write.sh path -- never a second writer.
- Preserve plan-file-scope-harvest.sh's exit-code contract: exit 0 with `[]` for "nothing to harvest" is a NORMAL outcome and must never become an error; non-zero stays reserved for genuine usage errors.
- plan-file-scope-harvest.sh's header comment and context/formats/plan-format.md's field definition are documented as mirroring each other verbatim. Any grammar change must update both, or the mirror claim becomes false.
- If the harvester's grammar widens, re-check the three heading-name-stability-only consumers its header names, plus validate-artifact.sh's per-phase presence check, which is documented as the ONE place that distinguishes "no header" from "header present, zero paths".

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-plan-file-scope-harvest.sh with a fixture reproducing the actual rendering from the cited plan that failed to harvest (hypothesis (a)), and one pinning the path-form question (hypothesis (b)) in whichever direction the ruling goes.
- Extend scripts/tests/test-git-commit-phase.sh with fixtures pinning: a genuine out-of-territory path is STILL dropped with its warning AND its issue-record call; the fixed-scope carve-out paths are still staged unconditionally; fail-open on absent/empty/malformed file_scope is unchanged; a .claude/** path is still dropped; and the PART B surfacing actually fires and is consumable.
- Run the plan-postflight harvest end to end against a fixture plan, confirming the merged file_scope equals the plan's authoritative list -- not only that the script exits 0.

TERRITORY EDGES (declared per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md -- shared file territory is serialized with a dependency edge, never excluded). Each edge below is labelled with its rationale, because a bare edge conflates semantic prerequisite with mere serialization:
- 430 (SEMANTIC + file overlap, the strongest edge): it owns git-commit-phase.sh, test-git-commit-phase.sh, context/standards/git-staging-scope.md and agents/general-implementation-agent.md, and it rules the implement-time feedback channel (whether proposed_file_scope extends to implement, auto-merge versus operator confirmation, and what the operator sees). This record's PART B surfacing must MATCH that ruling rather than invent a second one, and its cause (d) above defers to it outright. Must land first.
- 443 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/tests/test-git-commit-phase.sh. No semantic interaction.
- 407 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes scripts/skill-base.sh. No semantic interaction with the harvest branch.
- 385 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes context/formats/plan-format.md. No semantic interaction.
- 328 (SERIALIZATION only): its file_scope covers agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/, context/, agents/ and rules/ at DIRECTORY level, so it overlaps nearly everything here; it is a net-deletion corpus consolidation that would churn these same files. Ordering it first avoids re-consolidating this record's output. An operator who confirms 328's consolidation does not touch these specific files may lift this edge with update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 430 was read in FULL and rejected as an absorption target: it is explicitly implement-dispatch-scoped ("whether an implement dispatch should propose additions to its own task's file_scope"), its three scope questions never consider the accepted plan's "Files to modify" list as an input, and its own duplicate-check section lists the three neighbours it rejected without reaching the plan-acceptance stage. This record's intervention point is the plan postflight and the harvester's grammar, upstream of every drop 430 reacts to. 425 (completed) carved ENGINE-written shared state files out of the sibling predicate in orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh -- a different script and a disjoint cause. 409 acts before dispatch on dirty-tree-versus-declaration, not declaration-versus-plan. An edge on 430 was declared instead of a merge.
PARTS A AND B WERE DELIBERATELY COMBINED, not split: they are the two halves of one loop (what the declaration says, and whether anyone is required to read it when the declaration still falls short), five of the six citations are shared evidence, and splitting them would produce two records both rewriting the same containment prose and both needing separate reconciliation against 430.

### 445. Gate8 expected vs new failure distinction
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 414, Task 415
- **Parent**: Task 424

**Description**: Give verify-deploy.sh's Gate 8 an expected-versus-new failure distinction instead of collapsing any non-zero run-all.sh exit to FAIL.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/**; both are permitted in this description.

--- WHY ---

Gate 8 wraps tests/run-all.sh and treats ANY non-zero exit as a gate failure, with no distinction between a known-expected failure and a genuinely new one. But run-all.sh already computes and prints exactly that distinction itself, in its own summary line, e.g.:

    158 passed, 1 failed (1 expected, 0 NEW), 0 skipped, 159 total

So the information Gate 8 needs is already produced by its own child and then discarded.

--- MEASURED EVIDENCE ---

Observed live during a multi-task orchestration batch. A phase's Gate 8 reported FAIL whose sole cause was the pre-existing, out-of-scope test-gate-cache-equivalence.sh EXPECTED failure. Confirmed by a clean isolated run-all.sh at quiet load: 158 passed, 1 failed (1 expected, 0 NEW) -- zero new failures. The phase had to be closed [COMPLETED WITH EXCLUSIONS] with a hand-written Reasoned Exclusions record, purely to explain a gate verdict that its own child had already correctly characterized as containing no new failure.

This recurred across the same batch: every full gate-set run in that invocation came back non-zero on Gate 8 alone, each time requiring a human or agent to re-derive by hand that the failing set was the known-expected one. Independently, two other suites (test-lint-state-writer-boundary.sh, test-update-task-status.sh) failed transiently under contended load in one pass and were confirmed load-induced noise only via isolated re-runs -- the same manual re-derivation cost, for the same missing distinction.

--- GOAL ---

Gate 8's verdict should reflect run-all.sh's own expected/NEW accounting rather than a bare exit code: a run whose only failures are the declared expected set is not the same event as a run with a new failure, and the gate should not force every consumer to re-establish that by hand.

Required properties:
- Consume run-all.sh's existing expected/NEW accounting rather than recomputing or re-parsing it independently. The producer already owns this; do not fork a second source of truth.
- Preserve the anchored finding-extraction contract. Gate 8's findings are selected with an anchored match on run-all.sh's line-initial verdict rows; do not regress that anchoring, and do not let a NEW-versus-expected change reintroduce an unanchored substring match.
- Keep a new failure blocking. This task is about not crying wolf on the expected set, never about weakening the gate: a NEW failure must still fail Gate 8.
- Decide and state explicitly whether an all-expected failing run exits zero, or exits non-zero with a distinguishable status/output that downstream consumers can read. Name the chosen semantics in the plan and justify it against the consumers below.

--- CONSUMERS TO RE-VERIFY (the verdict is shared, so this is not Gate 8's problem alone) ---

Gate 8's FINDINGS_LIST feeds deploy_findings_snapshot (scripts/lib/deploy-baseline-lib.sh), whose callers include scripts/orchestrate-batch-gate.sh, scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh's inter-cycle redeploy checkpoint (whose pre/post comparison decides whether an orchestration batch PROCEEDS), scripts/command-gate-out.sh, and verify-deploy.sh itself. Confirm for each that a changed Gate 8 verdict or output shape does not silently alter a gating decision, and in particular that a pre/post baseline comparison cannot be skewed by the expected set appearing in one capture and not the other.

--- COORDINATION CONSTRAINT ---

verify-deploy.sh is contested territory. A separate in-flight effort is refactoring the gate set and suite runner into a declarative, parallel gate framework with a gate registry, per-gate timeouts and a uniform cost model, and it owns verify-deploy.sh and tests/run-all.sh. This task must be SEQUENCED after that work rather than run concurrently with it, and should be implemented on top of the registry if the registry has landed by then -- adding a gate property rather than hand-patching an inlined per-gate block. Check the state of that refactor before planning, and add a dependencies[] edge if it is still open.

--- VERIFICATION ---

- A fixture run whose only failure is a declared-expected suite, asserting the chosen Gate 8 semantics.
- A fixture run containing a genuinely NEW failure, asserting Gate 8 still fails.
- A non-vacuousness check per assertion: deliberately break the distinction and confirm the assertion bites.
- Re-verification of each consumer named above against both fixture shapes.
- Final phase declares the complete gate set with an explicit source-store TARGET.

Severity: minor in isolation, but it taxes every phase closure in the repository, and its cost is paid in hand-written Reasoned Exclusions records that obscure real regressions.
